## Executor, round 9 (round 25, agent 160)

Step 1: nested-loop rescan and wider batch kernels (HANDOFF "Executor, round 9, step 1").
**base** is the branch at `3df0c607`; **new** is this step on top of it. Both are dist builds
(fat LTO) of their own checkouts.

How it was run:
- Each server is in a container of its own, both pinned to the same four CPUs (4-7) and
  both in system.slice. The client is on 8-11, at nice 19.
- PostgreSQL 18.6 is the pinned image with the bench settings (shared_buffers 2 GB, host
  network). The engine has `--cache-mb 2048`, with its binary bind-mounted
  (`run.sh ENGINE_DOCKER=1`).
- Rounds are interleaved: PostgreSQL, new, base, base, new, PostgreSQL. Each engine run is
  a fresh server, a warm-up pass, then the measured one.
- User-space instructions and cycles come from `perf stat -e instructions:u,cycles:u` of the
  engine process, from a privileged helper container. CPU is the cgroup's.
- The machine ran at load 15-40. Wall times move about ±10% from run to run.

### 200,000 inner index lookups (`bench nl`: hash and merge joins off, serial; 1M-row `nl_t`, primary key; median of 5)

| query | PostgreSQL | base | new |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sum(t.v) FROM generate_series(1, 200000) g JOIN nl_t t ON t.id = g` | 0.23, 0.26 s | 0.84, 0.85 s | **0.68, 0.69 s** (-19%) |
| `LEFT JOIN nl_t t ON t.id = g * 7` | 0.24, 0.27 s | 0.70, 0.71 s | **0.55, 0.58 s** (-19%) |
| `EXISTS (... WHERE t.id = g * 3)` | 0.20, 0.21 s | 0.60, 0.61 s | **0.44, 0.44 s** (-27%) |
| user instructions, 10^9 (the 3 queries, 5 runs each) | | 80.7, 81.2 | **68.4, 69.6 (-15%)** |
| user cycles, 10^9 | | 43.5, 43.9 | **34.8, 35.3 (-20%)** |
| peak (engine VmHWM) | | 173-175 MB | 171-173 MB |

The inner join is 2.8x PostgreSQL's time, down from 3.4x (round 8 measured 3.2x on a release
build). The rest is the storage lookup, which agent 149's point-lookup path behind `next`
takes on: a TxnScan per rescan, the entry seek, the row fetch.

### TPC-H SF1 (all 22 over the wire)

| | PostgreSQL | base | new |
|---|---|---|---|
| wall, s (sum of the 22) | 12.41, 11.98 | 19.58, 18.88 | **17.02, 17.63** |
| CPU, s (cgroup) | 29.1, 29.6 | 45.5, 45.2 | **41.6, 42.7** |
| user instructions, 10^9 | | 303.4, 305.2 | **280.3, 281.4 (-7.7%)** |
| user cycles, 10^9 | | 176.8, 175.3 | **163.1, 162.7 (-7.5%)** |
| peak, MB (engine VmHWM; PostgreSQL's anon + shmem) | 1,775, 1,775 | 1,832, 1,832 | 1,836, 1,829 |

All 22 result hashes equal PostgreSQL's in every run.

**Engine/PostgreSQL (new): wall 1.42x, CPU 1.44x, peak 1.03x.** Round 8's 2.4x / 1.9x / 1.44x
came from release builds, and since then from size models, scan cost rounds and query memory.
The base here measures 1.58x / 1.54x.

Per query, seconds (the round's medians):

| | q01 | q10 | q12 | q13 | q18 | q21 | q22 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PostgreSQL | 1.98 | 0.65 | 0.60 | 0.73 | 2.53 | 0.62 | 0.10 |
| base | 1.37 | 1.31 | 1.63 | 1.62 | 2.04 | 1.45 | 0.86 |
| new | **1.23** | **0.82** | **1.31** | **1.39** | **2.00** | **0.96** | **0.43** |

- q01 and q18 are ahead of PostgreSQL.
- q10 and q12 read lineitem through filters on char(n) columns (`l_returnflag = 'R'`,
  `l_shipmode IN (...)`). Under the binder's `::bpchar` cast, neither the
  stored comparisons nor the batch filter took them, so every row was decoded and filtered
  row by row. The cast now compiles away.
- q21 and q22 are nested-loop semi and anti joins over inner index scans: lineitem l2 / l3
  by `lineitem_pkey`, orders by `orders_o_custkey_idx`. They are rescanned now, and stop at
  the first match, as do the workers' lookups.
- q13 gains from LIKE becoming a kernel: orders' `o_comment NOT LIKE` filter runs in the
  workers (`LikePattern::matches` is 5% of its profile). What's left is the grouping above
  the join (`AggNode::run`, 18%).

### pgbench and the footprint gate (`scoreboard.sh quick`, SB_ONLY=pgbench,analytic: 2 interleaved rounds, scale 1)

User instructions per transaction (`perf stat` of the engine; scale 10, `-M prepared`, 8
clients, 15 s after a 5 s warm-up, a fresh data directory each; new, base, base, new, two
rounds):

| | base | new |
|---|---|---|
| select-only | 82.7k-82.9k | 82.8k-83.0k |
| simple-update (`-N`) | 366k-397k | 375k-397k |
| tpcb-like | 878k-910k | 863k-904k |

These are unchanged: point reads and updates don't take the changed paths.

Engine/PostgreSQL (scale 1, 2 rounds, CPU per operation; above 1x the engine is ahead):

| | base | new |
|---|---|---|
| select-only, 1 / 8 / 32 clients | 0.99x / 0.81x / 0.89x | 0.98x / 0.83x / 0.89x |
| tpcb-like, 1 / 8 / 32 clients | 0.88x / 0.90x / 1.17x | 0.83x / 0.82x / 1.17x |

The two scoreboard runs were not interleaved and the machine's load moved between them. The
instruction counts above are the measure.

Footprint gate (new):
- fresh data directory 3.03 MB (PostgreSQL 38.9);
- idle CPU over 5 minutes 0.02 s (0.54);
- 100 idle sessions 29.9 MB (211).

All three pass. The step adds no thread, timer or cache. The inner node it keeps lives as
long as its join.

## Executor, round 8 (round 25, agent 154)

Step 1 of batch execution (HANDOFF "Executor: batch execution, the next step", "What is

against the executor's stub `decode_cols`. **base** is the branch at `847fbdba`; **new** is
this step on top of it. Both are release builds of their own checkouts.

How it was run:
- Each server is in a container of its own at nice 19, both pinned to the same four CPUs
  (20-23), both in system.slice. PostgreSQL 18.6 is the pinned image with the bench
  settings (shared_buffers 2 GB); the engine has `--cache-mb 2048`.
- TPC-H data: `bench tpch-load --sf 1` on both.
- Rounds are interleaved: PostgreSQL, new, base, base, new, PostgreSQL.
- The machine ran at load 15-45 throughout. User-space instructions (`perf stat`) are the
  measure; wall times move about ±10% from run to run.
- Before the rebase onto `847fbdba`, a host-run engine competing in user.slice took 75-100 s
  for the 22 queries. Those runs aren't comparable, and none of their numbers are used here.

### Instructions per row, in process (`scan_bench sql`, compacted, 1M rows; 3 reps plus a warm-up, minus a run with none, over 4 runs' rows)

Per row, the batch path's work is the stub decode (by tag, values copied), the typed
aggregate loop and the per-batch filter. Agent 149's typed `decode_cols` replaces the stub.

| base | 1.97 | 1.00 | 0.76 | 1.57 | 0.54 | 1.50 |
| new | **1.37** | 0.95 | 0.71 | **1.21** | 0.49 | **1.14** |

- q01 is now ahead of PostgreSQL (0.66x). It is a lineitem aggregation with bpchar keys, and
  its six aggregates are taken from the batches.
- q17 and q21 gain from the probe lookups: lineitem rows whose key misses the hash table are
  dropped before a row is made.
- The other queries move ±10% in both directions with the machine.
- q06 is a Parallel Bitmap Heap Scan. Its rows come one at a time from index chunks, which
  keep the row path.

### pgbench (scale 10, `-M prepared`, 8 clients, 15 s after a 5 s warm-up, a fresh copy of one database each; base, new, new, base, two rounds)

| | base | new |
|---|---|---|
| select-only: user instructions per transaction | 89.7k, 88.9k | 88.7k, 89.0k |
| select-only: engine CPU ms / 1,000 transactions | 66, 58, 58, 56 | 70, 72, 56, 67 |
| tpcb-like: user instructions per transaction | 1,149k, 1,146k | 1,122k (one run's perf attach failed) |
| tpcb-like: engine CPU ms / 1,000 transactions | 702, 704, 586, 541 | 648, 660, 673, 657 |
| peak RSS | 222-261 MB | 224-254 MB |

The instruction counts are unchanged. Point reads and updates don't take the batch path.
The CPU-time spread runs in both directions between rounds.

### Footprint gate (a fresh engine in its container; `bench idle --mem-pid`)

| | base | new |
|---|---|---|
| fresh data directory | 56 kB | 56 kB |
| idle CPU over 300 s | 0.01 s | 0.02 s (one 10 ms tick) |
| 100 idle sessions: memory, `base_mb` | 44.1 MB | 45.5 MB |
| per session | 325 kB | 328 kB |

The step adds no thread, timer or cache. Its buffers are per chunk and are freed with the
chunk.

### tpcb allocations, attributed (for agent 145)

The base build (`847fbdba`) with frame pointers (`RUSTFLAGS=-C force-frame-pointers=yes`,
`CFLAGS=-fno-omit-frame-pointer`). pgbench tpcb-like, 8 clients, `perf record -F 499 -g`
for 15 s: 39,159 samples. Each sample whose leaf is mimalloc's malloc or free is counted
against the first two engine frames above the allocator (`perf script`, then a fold).

- The allocator is **8.7%** of the samples (`_mi_page_malloc_zero` alone 5.5%). The rest is
  a long tail.
- Rows decoded into `Vec<Value>` by the scans (`scan::scan_row_need`, `scan_row_rest`, from
  `ScanNode::next`): ~1.4%.
- Each statement's storage scan setup (`storage::hot::Hot::scan` from `Store::scan_raw`,
  `Txn::scan_snap`, `Txn::toast_source`, `TxnScan::fill_batch`): ~1.05%.
- Per statement in the session (`Session::execute_statement`, `run_query`,
  `server::message_loop` / `handle`, and the search path built twice, by
  `Settings::session_search_path` and `ExecCtx::with_search_path`): ~1.9%.
- Heap-order reads of the small modelled tables (`ScanNode::next_heap_ordered`): 0.47%.
- `UpdateNode::next` 0.27%, `nodes::open_node` 0.14%.

The decode is what batch execution removes for scans. The rest belongs to OLTP: the
statement path and the scan setup.

## Executor, round 7 (round 24, agent 143)

Release builds with line tables. **base** is the branch at `7c1f72b3`; **batch 1** is this
round's first batch on top of it. One engine per run, PostgreSQL 18.6 the pinned image with
the bench settings, on the host network. The machine ran at load ~40 of 36 CPUs with every
job at nice 19, so wall times are loose; user-space instructions and cycles (`perf stat -e
instructions:u,cycles:u` of the engine process) are the measure.

### TPC-H SF1 (all 22 over the wire; a fresh engine each, a warm-up run, then the measured one)

| | base | batch 1 |
|---|---|---|
| user instructions, 10^9 | 427.7, 428.5 | 423.1, 423.8 (-1.1%) |
| user cycles, 10^9 | 240.9, 240.9 | 232.3, 227.4 (-4.6%) |
| peak (VmHWM) | 2,128, 2,122 MB | 2,141, 2,144 MB (+0.8%) |

All 22 result hashes equal PostgreSQL's in every run. The peak's growth is the blocks'
restart numbers (4 bytes per 16 entries, every block of TPC-H's 1.4 GB in the cache).

### q20 (in process, 15 warm runs, interleaved)

| | base | batch 1 |
|---|---|---|
| median | 0.429, 0.439 s | 0.360, 0.374 s (-16%) |
| peak | 1,077 MB | 1,086 MB |

Where q20's fetches went (share of user cycles, warm):

| | base | batch 1 |
|---|---|---|
| `find_block` (in `get_versions_with`) + its key compares | ~15% | 5.3% |
| `Block::seek` / `read` / `entry_head` | 15% | ~13% |
| `BlockCache::get` | 4.0% | 5.7% |

What is left is memory latency: a point read's block, its restart array and its cache entry
are seldom in the CPU's cache.

### pgbench (scale 10, `-M prepared`, 8 clients, 15 s after a 5 s warm-up, fresh engine; base, batch 1, batch 1, base)

| | base | batch 1 |
|---|---|---|
| select-only: engine CPU ms / 1,000 transactions | 66, 69 | 67, 66 |
| simple-update | 313, 320 | 312, 322 |
| tpcb-like | 605, 641 | 597, 619 |
| peak RSS | 438, 459 MB | 439, 470 MB |
| TPS select-only / tpcb-like | 83.1k, 79.2k / 9,861, 9,305 | 79.8k, 82.5k / 9,749, 9,751 |

Unchanged (within the spread between rounds of one build).

### Batch 2 (measured on its pre-push build `6ce5d037`, before the recovery's additions)

Instructions per row, in process (`scan_bench sql`, compacted, 1M rows; a run of 3 reps
minus one with none, `perf stat -e instructions:u`). **base** is `7c1f72b3`; nothing in
batch 1 touches these paths.

| query | base w0 | batch 2 w0 | base w2 | batch 2 w2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `count(*) FROM n` | 458 | 458 | 1,355 | **883** |
| `sum(b) FROM n` | 1,679 | **1,201** | 1,669 | **1,206** |
| `count(*) FROM n WHERE b = 7` | 1,642 | 1,630 | 2,132 | 1,911 |
| `sum(h) FROM w` | 2,436 | **1,958** | 2,465 | **1,967** |
| `sum(b) FROM m` | 1,762 | **1,285** | 1,764 | **1,285** |

Serial `sum(b)` is 81 ns of CPU a row on this machine. PostgreSQL's sequential scan plus
`sum` is 60-80 ns, so the engine is at about 1.0-1.3x PostgreSQL here, against about 1.8x
before. The serial filtered `count(*)` runs through `ScanNode` (`count_rest`), not
`parscan`, so it is unchanged.

TID scans, 100,000 rows, pgbench `-M prepared -c 1`, 5 s, TPS:

| | base (`7c1f72b3`) | batch 2 |
|---|---|---|
| `ctid = '(5,7)'` (a live row) | 4,749 | 14,407 |
| `ctid = '(0,1)'` (an older version) | 14.4 | **15,561** |
| `ctid = '(9999,1)'` (no tuple) | 14.4 | **14,875** |
| `UPDATE ... WHERE ctid = (SELECT ctid ... WHERE id = :id)` | 3,322 | 5,583 |

Batch 2 makes the older-version and missing-TID rows. Agent 142's per-TID `keys_at`
(c97af248, in between) makes the live-row and UPDATE rows.

TPC-H SF1 on batch 2, over the wire: all 22 result hashes equal PostgreSQL's. The 22 queries
take 24.2 s on the engine against 12.2 s on PostgreSQL (wall, one run each, at load ~40),
so the **engine/PostgreSQL ratio is about 2.0x**. Peak is 2,002 MB against PostgreSQL's
1.6 GB. q20 takes 0.47 s against 0.12 s.

Full verification of `6ce5d037`, before the recovery's additions:
- crate tests in debug and release (crash_random seeds 1-30), with clippy showing no new warning;
- the difftest smoke;
- isolation: 134 / 136 specs and 1,749 / 1,751 permutations, as on origin;
- regress, on a private reference: 51,148 / 51,150 with 0 units changed;
- pg_dump_tap: 14,817 / 14,817.

## Round 23 (agent 121): executor speed and memory, round 5

Same harness and settings; both servers on cores 28-31, the client on 6-7. **pg** is
PostgreSQL 18.6 (`a121-pg`); **base** the branch at `ff187f1f`, **new** batch 1 on top. Wall
times and even CPU seconds varied 1.5x between rounds of one binary (load 2 to 50 over the
session), so the primary measure is the engine's **user-space instructions** per query
(`perf stat -e instructions:u` on the engine process, a fresh engine, a warm-up run of the
queries first, one run each), which repeat within 1-2% between runs. Every result hash
agrees with PostgreSQL's in every round.

### Batch 1: narrowed rows, scans of the files in range, row fetches, numeric sizes

| | base | new |
|---|---|---|
| TPC-H, all 22, user instructions (10^9) | 455.6 | **448.5** (-1.6%) |
| q02 | 5.49 | **4.94** (-10%) |
| q14 | 3.94 | **2.93** (-25%) |
| q18 | 73.4 | **67.6** (-8%) |
| q07 / q03 / q12 | 10.8 / 17.3 / 42.2 | 10.2 / 16.7 / 41.2 |
| peak memory (VmHWM over the run, MB) | 2,089 | 2,064 |

Two interleaved full rounds (base, new, new, base; time s / CPU s): base 46.1 / 107.1 and
36.1 / 89.5, new 53.7 / 115.6 (during a load spike: q01, which this batch doesn't change,
took 4.1 s against 2.2-3.0) and 35.7 / 89.7; PostgreSQL 16.2 / 38.7. Peak 1,992-1,995 MB base,
1,968-1,985 new, PostgreSQL 1,612. On the batch's first base (`3f56fe3f`, before agent 105's
hot ranges and agent 117's scan work landed) the same changes took q02 7.0 -> 5.6, q10
54.7 -> 38.5 and q20 3.8 -> 3.0 (10^9 instructions; a profile of that base's q10 had
`Cursor::head` and `Merge::min` at 22%, the merge of a dozen small L0 files per scan). The
rebased base already runs q10 in 40.2, so upstream's work since covers most of that share.

**pgbench select-only** (scale 10, `-M prepared`, 8 clients, 10 s, fresh engine each, base /
new / base / new, cores 24-27): 65.8k / 67.9k / 65.9k / 65.7k TPS, 110.4k / 109.3k / 111.6k /
110.0k user instructions per transaction, VmHWM 199-201 MB each.

- **Narrowed rows** (`exec/nodes.rs` `Narrow`, `exec::collect_need`, `pipe::Built::narrowed`):
  a full sort keeps only the columns read above it and by its keys; a hash join's inner side
  is read with the mask of the columns its output, condition and keys read and its rows
  narrowed as they arrive (before, every inner row was collected whole, all its columns
  decoded, and compacted afterwards); a nested loop's inner side is read with its mask (q02's
  partsupp fetches no longer decode `ps_comment`).
- **Range scans merge only the files in range** (`storage::sources`): a scan had a cursor
  per L0 file and the whole of L1, and `Merge::min` compared every source's head per entry
  (landed concurrently in agent 117's 184f719f, which the measurements' base predates; after
  the rebase the files are compared by user keys).
- **Row fetches skip L1's bloom filters** (`Store::get_versions`): the keys come from index
  entries and reach L1 only when no newer source had them.
- **numeric sizes without text** (`relsize::small_numeric_digits`): a hash table's tuple
  lengths (`minimal_len`, where buckets are rebuilt) and the size models counted a numeric's
  base-10000 groups by formatting it.

### What holds memory (measured, not changed)

A fresh engine with `--cache-mb 64`, after q03, then q10's joins alone (j2) and q10 (j3):
VmHWM +110 MB and +125 MB. A heap-growth profile (a counting global allocator logging the
allocating stack every 2 MB past the live peak; a throwaway build) put j3's growth in the
block cache's fill, customer's rows read on the executor's thread for heap order
(`scan::heap_order`, the rows, keys and TID vectors: ~40 MB) and allocator slack: RSS 545
MB against a live peak of 372 MB. With `MIMALLOC_PURGE_DELAY=0` the same run peaked 40 MB
lower and ended at 273 MB RSS instead of 536. q10's 37-column Sort isn't run (its grouping
is partial in the workers), so narrowing sorts doesn't move the TPC-H peak; it is the cache
(~1.45 GB) plus per-query working memory that PostgreSQL bounds by work_mem and spills.

### Where time still goes

- Point lookups (q02, q20): per fetch a block-cache lookup, a block seek and the merge of
  sources; q20's 8,332 SubPlan runs open the inner plan anew each time.
- Above unfiltered scans (agent 117): a `Vec<Value>` per row and expression evaluation;
  `sum` 2,331 instructions a row against 1,067 for `count(*)` (`examples/scan_bench.rs`).
- Not done: streaming merge joins, parallel hash builds, gather-merge sorts, right-join
  groupings in the workers.

### Verification (round 23, agent 121, batch 1)

- `cargo test -p pg-engine` in debug (before the last rebase) and release (after; deep_input
  and the parallel tests included), `cargo clippy -p pg-engine --all-targets` with no warning
  in the files changed, the difftest smoke, the isolation suite 121 / 122 specs (1,647 /
  1,648 permutations), the regression suite in full (debug, private reference) 51,148 /
  51,150 with no unit changed against `results.tsv`; golden `a121_narrow_rows`, unit test
  `relsize::tests::small_numeric_digits_match_text`.

## Round 22 (agent 91): partial paths in the join search

The same plan comparison as round 21's (`EXPLAIN` of the 22 TPC-H queries at SF1, PostgreSQL
18.6 in `a91-pg` and the engine loaded with the same data by `tools/bench` and analyzed).
q05 and q08 now have PostgreSQL's parallel plans: the join search keeps partial paths per
join relation, and the cheapest partial path's tree (customer x nation x region in the
workers, orders by orders_o_custkey_idx, for q05; customer by customer_pkey for q08) is the
one EXPLAIN's parallel plans follow (STATUS.md round 22). With the engine restarted before
the run: **19 of 22** parallel (q13 differs after a restart, agent 85's; q18, q21 as before),
**19 of 22** serial (q18, q21; q10 by ANALYZE's sample). Every result hash agrees with
PostgreSQL's. `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` shows (and runs) the same plan: q05's partial tree with
its own row counts.

**Planning time.** CPU time of the engine process per statement, 2,000 statements per query
in one session, the base (`69743b6c`) and this batch interleaved three times, the lower of
each (read from `/proc/<pid>/stat`, which the machine's load of 20-30 moves less than wall
time, whose run-to-run spread was 30% per query):

| workload | base | batch | batch / base |
|---|---|---|---|
| pgbench's statements and ten ORM-style queries over pgbench_accounts (scale 10), EXPLAINed | 8,665 us | 8,545 us | 0.99 |
| the same, executed (the eight that run in under a millisecond) | 15,415 us | 15,245 us | 0.99 |
| TPC-H's 22 queries, EXPLAINed (200 each) | 140.3 ms | 141.9 ms | 1.01 |

OLTP planning is unchanged: a join of a lookup to a big table makes its leaves' partial scans
(from the serial paths already made) and stops there, since no parallel plan could beat the
serial one by the bound in `partial_pass`. The first version, which made the partial join
paths inline and named the partial trees' scans by a walk of every plan in EXPLAIN, cost
12% more CPU per OLTP join statement (38% for a three-way join); both are gone. The TPC-H
queries that run the pass pay for PostgreSQL's search, less the join pairs whose outer side's
cheapest partial path already costs more than the bound (any partial join over it costs at
least that: without that, the TPC-H total was 1.14x, q05 1.50x): q05 13.9 ms -> 15.2 ms
(1.09x), q08 23.3 -> 24.7 (1.06x), q07 and q09 the same. Per-query noise is about 20% at this
load; the TPC-H row is a later run than the OLTP rows, at another load.

Memory: the pass's partial paths live with the search (as the serial ones); a skipped pass
keeps one record per join pair (its relations and clause list), dropped with the search.

## Round 23 (agent 116): plan shapes against PostgreSQL, ORM and TPC-DS-style queries

`tools/bench/plancmp.py` runs `EXPLAIN` of query sets on PostgreSQL 18.6 and the engine and
reports, per query, whether the plan's node shapes match and whether the text matches with
costs left out (PostgreSQL's JIT block dropped), with the engine's total cost over
PostgreSQL's; `SUMMARY shape=N/M` is the number to start from. Sets: the 22 TPC-H queries,
`tools/bench/plans/orm.sql` (45 queries as Django, Rails, Prisma, EF Core, Hibernate and
SQLAlchemy write them: lookups, counts, pages, prefetches, EXISTS / IN, LATERAL loads,
keyset pages, group counts) and `plans/tpcds.sql` (18 TPC-DS-style queries), all over TPC-H
SF1 loaded by `tools/bench` into both (`a116-pg`, the engine restarted before each run):

```sh
python3 tools/bench/plancmp.py --pg-port <pg> --engine-port <engine> --db tpch \
  --sets tpch,orm,tpcds [--serial] [--show q18,orm:keyset_page] [--diff]
```

| plan shapes matching | base (`6423b044`) | this batch |
|---|---|---|
| parallel query at its defaults | 69 / 89 (TPC-H 19 / 22) | **77 / 89** (TPC-H 19 / 22) |
| `max_parallel_workers_per_gather = 0` | 78 / 89 (TPC-H 19 / 22) | **80 / 89** (TPC-H 19 / 22) |

(The base measured 71 / 89 parallel in an earlier run: q13 and a near-tie flip with the
restart's heap models.) What changed is in STATUS.md round 23. Left (HANDOFF item 11):
TPC-H q13 (the restored heap model's pages), q18 and q10 serial (group-useful pathkeys at
joins), q21 (Gather paths at join relations); ORM `keyset_page` and
`exists_limit_order_limit` (Gather (Merge) choices under a LIMIT), `efcore_select_many`,
`prisma_lateral_join` (a LATERAL item's Memoize), `cte_window`; TPC-DS `rollup_nation_year`,
`except_parts`, `top_customers_by_year`, `union_channels`. Every TPC-H result hash agrees
with PostgreSQL's (reps 1, after a restart).

**Planning time.** CPU time of the engine process per statement (`/proc/<pid>/stat`), the
base and this batch interleaved three times, the engine restarted and warmed before each
(a machine at load 35-45, the engine on four pinned cores). EXPLAIN of pgbench's
statements and ten ORM-style queries over pgbench_accounts (scale 10, 1,000 each), the
cheap ones of them executed (400 each), the 45 ORM queries over TPC-H (100 each), the 22
TPC-H queries (100 each):

| workload | base (3 runs) | batch (3 runs) | batch / base, lower of each | median |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OLTP + ORM over pgbench, EXPLAINed | 576 / 598 / 590 us | 530 / 655 / 523 us | 0.91 | 0.90 |
| the same, executed | 361 / 396 / 371 us | 357 / 441 / 268 us | 0.74 | 0.96 |
| ORM queries over TPC-H, EXPLAINed | 1,518 / 1,240 / 1,194 us | 1,320 / 1,510 / 976 us | 0.82 | 1.06 |
| TPC-H, EXPLAINed | 11.8 / 11.9 / 10.7 ms | 11.5 / 11.1 / 8.4 ms | 0.79 | 0.94 |

The spread between runs (up to 30%) is larger than any difference: planning CPU didn't
rise. Nothing in the batch runs on a plain OLTP plan: the new work is EXPLAIN-only (a
Limit's serial alternative, the target list over a relation's ordered alternatives, a
partial join's filter) or limited to join groups with a grouped FROM item (its outputs'
statistics, the order of a sorted grouping: one estimate of the item's plan). Peak memory
(VmHWM) was 349-357 MB for both.

pgbench (scale 10, 4 clients, 20 s, `-M prepared`, base / batch / base / batch): select-only
24,295 / 28,437 / 26,975 / 26,184 tps; tpcb-like 2,752 / 2,467 / 2,666 / 2,514 tps (synchronous
commits on a shared disk: the engine's CPU per transaction was 0.77-0.82 ms in all four), peak
memory 199-278 MB alike. No change on the statement path.

**Second batch: job-queue planning.** Agent 114 found Oban's and graphile-worker's fetches
slow by planning (2.3 / 1.8 ms to plan against PostgreSQL's 0.16 / 0.14). Measured here on
10,000-row tables (`tools/bench/access.rs`'s schema): PostgreSQL plans Oban's fetch in
0.26 ms warm (1.8 ms in a session's first statement), the engine in 1.6-1.7 ms. A profile
(a release build with frame pointers and line tables, perf from a privileged helper) put a
quarter of it in converting the `ANY_subquery` leaf with EXPLAIN's builder for paths it
doesn't have, and 4% in mergejoinscansel probing the index for actual column ranges, which
PostgreSQL doesn't do (STATUS.md round 23). After the batch, interleaved twice (EXPLAIN in a
session, 1,000 each; the warm `Planning Time` of 5 in a session):

| | first batch | second batch |
|---|---|---|
| Oban fetch, EXPLAIN CPU | 2,160 / 1,890 us | 1,510 / 1,050 us |
| Oban fetch, Planning Time (warm) | 1.67-1.84 ms | 0.99-1.10 ms |
| graphile-worker fetch, EXPLAIN CPU | 2,050 / 1,820 us | 1,610 / 1,360 us |

Planning CPU of the round's workloads, first batch / second batch interleaved three times
(lower of each; medians in brackets): OLTP + ORM over pgbench EXPLAINed 593 / 463 us (630 /
507), the same executed 373 / 361 us (396 / 368), ORM over TPC-H 1,174 / 980 us (1,264 /
1,014), TPC-H 9.7 / 7.0 ms (10.2 / 7.8): lower in every round. Plan shapes 77 / 89 as before;

| `max_parallel_workers_per_gather = 0` | 79 / 89 (TPC-H 19 / 22) | **81 / 89** (TPC-H 21 / 22) |

(Round 23 measured 80 / 89 serial; `orm:exists_limit_order_limit` flips between runs.) q10 and

(531 / 554 us); peak memory 229-311 MB alike. No change on the statement path: a plain SELECT
is analyzed then planned in one call, as before.

The queue-table DELETE agent 129 found slow (`DELETE ... WHERE hid = (SELECT min(hid) ...)`
after deleting the first 100,000 of 200,000 keys): 435-490 ms a statement before, as an
uncorrelated sub-select never bounded the index scan; now the InitPlan's value is a runtime
